# Double (association football)

This article is about "The Double" in football. For other uses, see [Double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_\(disambiguation\)).



[Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich)'s double-winning team of 2014

**The Double**, in [association football](10568.md), is the achievement of winning a country's top tier division and its primary [domestic cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_cup) competition in the same season. The lists in this article examine this definition of a double, while derivative sections examine much less frequent, continental instances. *The Double* can also mean beating a team both home and away in the same league season, a feat often noted as *doing the double* over a particular opponent.

The first club to achieve a double was [Preston North End](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preston_North_End_F.C.) in 1889, winning the [FA Cup](11237.md) and [The Football League](14419588.md) in the [inaugural season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1888%E2%80%9389_Football_League) of the league.

The team that holds the record for the most doubles is [Linfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linfield_F.C.) of [Northern Ireland](21265.md), with a total of 25.

[South Africa](17416221.md) and [Trinidad and Tobago](3565457.md) are the two countries with the most Double-winning clubs, with 13 clubs each.

### Armenia

Prior to the breakup of the Soviet Union, Armenian clubs were part of Soviet competition. [Ararat Yerevan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Ararat_Yerevan) was one of just two non-Russian clubs to win the Soviet double, in 1973, with [Nikita Simonyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikita_Simonyan) at the helm. They also won the post-independence Armenian Double in 1993.

In [Armenia](10918072.md), four teams have won the Double of the [Soviet Top League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Top_League)/[Armenian Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Premier_League) and the [Soviet Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Cup)/[Armenian Independence Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Independence_Cup).

| Club | Soviet Union | Armenia | Total |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Pyunik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Pyunik) | - | 2002, 2004, 2009, 2010, 2014–15 | 5 |
| [Ararat Yerevan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Ararat_Yerevan) | 1973 | 1993 | 2 |
| [Urartu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Urartu) | - | 2022–23 | 1 |
| [Noah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Noah) | - | 2024–25 | 1 |

### Czech Republic

Two teams won the Double of the [Czechoslovak First League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechoslovak_First_League) and [Czechoslovak Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechoslovak_Cup). Since the dissolution of [Czechoslovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechoslovakia) in 1993, the Double has consisted of the [Czech First League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_First_League) and [Czech Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Cup), being won by only two teams to date.

| Club | Czechoslovakia | Czech Republic | Total |

| [Sparta Prague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AC_Sparta_Prague) | 1983–84, 1987–88, 1988–89 | 2006–07, 2013–14, [2023–24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_AC_Sparta_Prague_season) | 6 |
| [Dukla Prague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dukla_Prague) | 1960–61, 1965–66 | - | 2 |
| [Slavia Prague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SK_Slavia_Prague) | - | [2018–19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_SK_Slavia_Prague_season), [2020–21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_SK_Slavia_Prague_season) | 2 |

### England

In England, eight teams have won the double of the [Football League First Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_League_First_Division) (1888–1992)/[Premier League](11250.md) (1992–present) and [FA Cup](11237.md).

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Manchester United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_United_F.C.) | 3 | [1993–94](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993%E2%80%9394_Manchester_United_F.C._season), [1995–96](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995%E2%80%9396_Manchester_United_F.C._season), [1998–99](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998%E2%80%9399_Manchester_United_F.C._season) |
| [Arsenal](2174.md) | 3 | [1970–71](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970%E2%80%9371_Arsenal_F.C._season), [1997–98](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997%E2%80%9398_Arsenal_F.C._season), [2001–02](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001%E2%80%9302_Arsenal_F.C._season) |
| [Manchester City](165813.md) | 2 | [2018–19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Manchester_City_F.C._season), [2022–23](70467955.md) |
| [Preston North End](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preston_North_End_F.C.) | 1 | [1888–89](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1888%E2%80%9389_Preston_North_End_F.C._season) |
| [Aston Villa](42173.md) | 1 | [1896–97](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1896%E2%80%9397_Aston_Villa_F.C._season) |
| [Tottenham Hotspur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tottenham_Hotspur_F.C.) | 1 | [1960–61](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1960%E2%80%9361_Tottenham_Hotspur_F.C._season) |
| [Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C.) | 1 | [1985–86](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985%E2%80%9386_Liverpool_F.C._season) |
| [Chelsea](7473.md) | 1 | [2009–10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_Chelsea_F.C._season) |

- [Preston North End](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preston_North_End_F.C.) in 1889 won the double without losing a game all season and without conceding any goals in their cup winning campaign.

- [Manchester United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_United_F.C.) won the double three times in the 1990s, including as part of a [continental treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)#Continental_trebles) in 1998–99.

- [Manchester City](165813.md) won the double as part of the first-ever [domestic treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)#Domestic_trebles) for an English men's football team in 2018–19, and as part of a continental treble in 2022–23.

### Germany

#### Inter-war period

A national cup competition was introduced in Germany in 1935, and was known as the [Tschammer-Pokal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DFB-Pokal#Tschammer-Pokal) for the country's then [minister of sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_von_Tschammer_und_Osten). Between 1935 and the suspension of cup play in 1944 because of World War II, the Double was won only once.

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Schalke 04](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Schalke_04) | 1 | 1937 |

#### Bundesliga era

Play for what had become the [DFB-Pokal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DFB-Pokal) was resumed following the war in 1953, and up until well after the formation of the [Bundesliga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bundesliga) in 1963, no club was able to complete the double. A new cup trophy was introduced as it was felt the previous one was associated with the Nazi period.

In the modern-day Bundesliga era, the double has been won by only five clubs. Bayern Munich holds the record, with 14.

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich) | 14 | [1968–69](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968%E2%80%9369_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [1985–86](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985%E2%80%9386_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [1999–2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999%E2%80%932000_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2002–03](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%9303_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2004–05](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004%E2%80%9305_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2005–06](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2007–08](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007%E2%80%9308_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2009–10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2012–13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2013–14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2015–16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2018–19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2019–20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_FC_Bayern_Munich_season), [2025–26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_FC_Bayern_Munich_season) |
| [1. FC Köln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1._FC_K%C3%B6ln) | 1 | 1977–78 |
| [Werder Bremen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SV_Werder_Bremen) | 1 | [2003–04](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003%E2%80%9304_SV_Werder_Bremen_season) |
| [Borussia Dortmund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borussia_Dortmund) | 1 | [2011–12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Borussia_Dortmund_season) |
| [Bayer Leverkusen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayer_04_Leverkusen) | 1 | [2023–24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_Bayer_04_Leverkusen_season) |

Note: Bayern Munich's 2013 and 2020 doubles were part of the club's [trebles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)) which also included their [UEFA Champions League](44220.md) victories.

Note: Bayer Leverkusen achieved the domestic double unbeaten.[^cite_note-1]

#### East Germany (defunct)

Following World War II, a separate football competition emerged in the [Soviet-occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_occupation_of_Germany) eastern area of Germany. The first division [DDR-Oberliga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DDR-Oberliga) was active from 1947–48 to 1990–91 and saw the introduction of the [FDGB-Pokal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FDGB-Pokal) in 1949. The cup competition was not staged in the 1950–51 season and the transitional 1955 and 1961 seasons. Five doubles were won in East German competition:

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Dynamo Dresden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamo_Dresden) | 3 | 1971, 1977, 1990 |
| [BFC Dynamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berliner_FC_Dynamo) | 1 | 1988 |
| [Hansa Rostock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F.C._Hansa_Rostock) | 1 | 1991 |

Since [the reunification of Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_reunification), no former East German club has won either the Bundesliga title or the DFB Pokal ([RB Leipzig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RB_Leipzig) having been founded in 2009); indeed, as of 2024, none of the aforementioned double winners are competing in the top flight.

### Luxembourg

In [Luxembourgish football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Luxembourg), the Double is made up of the [National Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxembourg_National_Division) title and the [Luxembourg Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxembourg_Cup). As there are no other senior football competitions in the country, and no club have never come close to winning any European tournament, the Double is the ultimate achievement for a Luxembourgish club in one season.

The most successful clubs are [Jeunesse Esch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeunesse_Esch) and [F91 Dudelange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F91_Dudelange) (one double 1947–48 as [Stade Dudelange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Dudelange)), who have completed eight Doubles each.

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Jeunesse Esch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeunesse_Esch) | 8 | 1936–37, 1953–54, 1972–73, 1973–74, 1975–76, 1987–88, 1996–97, 1998–99 |
| [F91 Dudelange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F91_Dudelange) | 8 | 1 as [Stade Dudelange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Dudelange): 1947–48 7 as F91 Dudelange: 2005–06, 2006–07, 2008–09, 2011–12, 2015–16, 2016–17, 2018–19 |
| [Differdange 03](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Differdange_03) | 4 | 3 as [Red Boys Differdange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FA_Red_Boys_Differdange): 1925–26, 1930–31, 1978–79 1 as Differdange 03: 2024–25 |
| [Avenir Beggen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Avenir_Beggen) | 3 | 1983–84, 1992–93, 1993–94 |
| [Fola Esch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CS_Fola_Esch) | 1 | 1923–24 |
| [Spora Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CA_Spora_Luxembourg) | 1 | 1927–28 |
| [Progrès Niedercorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Progr%C3%A8s_Niedercorn) | 1 | 1977–78 |
| [Union Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Luxembourg) | 1 | 1990–91 |
| [CS Grevenmacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CS_Grevenmacher) | 1 | 2002–03 |

### Portugal

In Portugal, the Double is called "*Dobradinha*" and is achieved by winning the [Primeira Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primeira_Liga) and the [Taça de Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta%C3%A7a_de_Portugal). Only the "[Big Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Three_\(Portugal\))" have done so since the nationwide round-robin league competition was introduced in 1934.

[Benfica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.L._Benfica) holds the record of eleven Doubles. [Sporting CP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sporting_CP) was the first side to achieve the Double, in 1941, as well as winning the most recent one, in 2025.

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Benfica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.L._Benfica) | 11 | 1942–43, 1954–55, 1956–57, 1963–64, 1968–69, [1971–72](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1971%E2%80%9372_S.L._Benfica_season), [1980–81](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980%E2%80%9381_S.L._Benfica_season), [1982–83](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982%E2%80%9383_S.L._Benfica_season), [1986–87](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986%E2%80%9387_S.L._Benfica_season), [2013–14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_S.L._Benfica_season), [2016–17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_S.L._Benfica_season) |
| [Porto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Porto) | 9 | 1955–56, 1987–88, 1997–98, [2002–03](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%9303_FC_Porto_season), [2005–06](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_FC_Porto_season), [2008–09](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%9309_FC_Porto_season), [2010–11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_FC_Porto_season), [2019–20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_FC_Porto_season), [2021–22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_FC_Porto_season) |
| [Sporting CP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sporting_CP) | 7 | 1940–41, 1947–48, 1953–54, 1973–74, 1981–82, [2001–02](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001%E2%80%9302_Sporting_CP_season), [2024–25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_Sporting_CP_season) |

### Republic of Ireland

In Ireland, the double is achieved by winning the [League of Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Ireland) and [FAI Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FAI_Cup). Bohemians managed a similar feat in 1928 season by winning the [League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Ireland), [FAI Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FAI_Cup), the [League of Ireland Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Ireland_Shield) and the [Leinster Senior Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leinster_Senior_Cup_\(association_football\)). [Derry City FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derry_City_FC) completed '[The Treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Treble#Domestic_trebles)' in 1988–89 by also winning the League of Ireland Cup.

| [Dundalk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dundalk_F.C.) | 4 | 1978–79, 1987–88, 2015, 2018 |
| [Bohemians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohemian_F.C.) | 3 | 1927–28, 2000–01, 2008 |
| [St James's Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_James's_Gate_F.C.) | 1 | 1921–22 |
| [Cork United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cork_United_F.C._\(1940%E2%80%931948\)) | 1 | 1940–41 |

| [Cork City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cork_City_F.C.) | 1 | 2017 |

### Romania

In [Romania](25445.md), the Double is called the Event and can be achieved by winning the [Liga I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liga_I) and the [Cupa României](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cupa_Rom%C3%A2niei). The team that has won the most doubles to date is [Steaua București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Steaua_Bucure%C8%99ti), with nine wins, followed by their [city rivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eternal_derby_\(Romania\)), [Dinamo București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti) with six wins. In recent times, [CFR Cluj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CFR_Cluj) has won the Double twice, being the fourth non-[Bucharest](36877.md) team to do so, after [Universitatea Craiova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CS_Universitatea_Craiova), [UTA Arad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_UTA_Arad) and [Ripensia Timişoara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Ripensia_Timi%C8%99oara).

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Steaua București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Steaua_Bucure%C8%99ti) | 9 | [1951](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1951_FC_Steaua_Bucure%C8%99ti_season), [1952](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_FC_Steaua_Bucure%C8%99ti_season), 1975–76, 1984–85, 1986–87, 1988–89, 1995–96, 1996–97, [2014–15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_FC_Steaua_Bucure%C8%99ti_season) |
| [Dinamo București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti) | 6 | [1963–64](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963%E2%80%9364_FC_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti_season), [1981–82](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981%E2%80%9382_FC_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti_season), [1983–84](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983%E2%80%9384_FC_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti_season), [1989–90](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989%E2%80%9390_FC_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti_season), 1999–2000, [2003–04](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003%E2%80%9304_FC_Dinamo_Bucure%C8%99ti_season) |
| [Universitatea Craiova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CS_Universitatea_Craiova) | 3 | 1980–81, 1990–91, 2025–26 |
| [CFR Cluj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CFR_Cluj) | 2 | 2007–08, 2009–10 |
| [UTA Arad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_UTA_Arad) | 1 | 1947–48 |

Note: [Steaua București](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Steaua_Bucure%C8%99ti) initially won the double in 1987–88 but voluntarily gave up their Cupa României title.

### Russia

Four Russian teams achieved the Double of the [Soviet Top League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Top_League) and the [USSR Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USSR_Cup) in the former Soviet Union. The final Soviet championship was contested in 1991 and, following the break-up of the Soviet Union, newly independent states organised their own national competitions, with UEFA regarding the [Russian Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Premier_League) and the [Russian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Cup_\(football\)) as the respective successors to the old Soviet league and USSR Cup.

| Club | Soviet Union | Russia | Total |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Spartak Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Spartak_Moscow) | 1938, 1939, 1958 | 1992, 1994, 1998 | 6 |
| [CSKA Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFC_CSKA_Moscow) | 1948, 1951, 1991 | [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_PFC_CSKA_Moscow_season), [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_PFC_CSKA_Moscow_season), [2012–13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_PFC_CSKA_Moscow_season) | 6 |
| [Zenit Saint Petersburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Zenit_Saint_Petersburg) | - | [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_FC_Zenit_Saint_Petersburg_season), [2019–20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_FC_Zenit_Saint_Petersburg_season), [2023–24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_FC_Zenit_Saint_Petersburg_season) | 3 |
| [Dynamo Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Dynamo_Moscow) | 1937 | - | 1 |
| [Torpedo Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Torpedo_Moscow) | 1960 | - | 1 |

### Scotland

Three teams have accomplished the double of the [Scottish league championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Scottish_football_champions) and the [Scottish Cup](883238.md). Only [Aberdeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeen_F.C.) have managed to break the [Old Firm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Firm) trend, during their stint of success in the 1980s.

### Sweden

In Sweden, six teams have won the Double of the [Swedish championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Swedish_football_champions) and the [Svenska Cupen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svenska_Cupen).

The Svenska Cupen was not introduced until the 1941 season, while Allsvenskan started for the 1923–24 season. Svenska Cupen was also not played between 1954 and 1966. It should also be considered that winning Allsvenskan did not grant the title of [Swedish champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Swedish_football_champions) until 1931 and between 1982 and 1992 when the title was given to the winner of other cup tournaments that were organized at the end of the league season.[^cite_note-5] The number of doubles for [IFK Göteborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFK_G%C3%B6teborg) and [Malmö FF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malm%C3%B6_FF) are affected by this. IFK Göteborg won the Swedish Championship together with Svenska Cupen in one additional season to the table below, they won the two titles in 1983, a year when they did not win Allsvenskan. Additionally while Malmö won Allsvenskan in 1989, they were not Swedish Championships for that season as they did not win the play-off following the ordinary league play. All other teams in the table below won the Swedish Championship in their double-winning seasons. The latest club to win a double is [Malmö FF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malm%C3%B6_FF) who completed the feat in 2024. In 1982, IFK Göteborg also managed to combine its double with winning the UEFA Cup.

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Malmö FF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malm%C3%B6_FF) | 8 | [1943–44](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1943%E2%80%9344_Malm%C3%B6_FF_season), 1950–51, 1952–53, 1967, 1974, 1975, 1986, [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Malm%C3%B6_FF_season) |
| [IFK Göteborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFK_G%C3%B6teborg) | 3 | 1982, 1983, 1991 |
| [IFK Norrköping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFK_Norrk%C3%B6ping) | 2 | 1942–43, 1944–45 |
| [Djurgårdens IF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djurg%C3%A5rdens_IF_Fotboll) | 2 | [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Djurg%C3%A5rdens_IF_season), [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Djurg%C3%A5rdens_IF_season) |
| [Helsingborgs IF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsingborgs_IF) | 2 | 1941, 2011 |
| [AIK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIK_Fotboll) | 1 | [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_AIK_Fotboll_season) |

### Ukraine

Prior to the breakup of the Soviet Union, Ukrainian clubs were part of Soviet competition. Since 1992, the country has had its own domestic competition. Dynamo Kyiv has four Soviet and nine Ukrainian Doubles among its honours.

In Ukraine, two teams have won the Double of the [Soviet Top League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Top_League)/[Ukrainian Premier League](2322171.md) and the [Soviet Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Cup)/[Ukrainian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Cup).

### Wales

In [Wales](69894.md), seven teams have won the Double of the [Division One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_Football_League_Division_One) (1904–92) / [Welsh Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_Premier_League) (1992–present) and the [Welsh Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_Cup). Achieving this and also winning the [Welsh League Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_League_Cup) would make a domestic [Treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Treble). To date, two clubs have achieved the Treble. Rhyl achieved a domestic quadruple in 2003–04, winning the Welsh Premier, Welsh Cup, Welsh Premier League Cup and North Wales Challenge Cup. They narrowly missed out on a clean sweep of five trophies losing the Welsh Premier Cup Final to Wrexham.

## South America

### Argentina

[Boca Juniors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boca_Juniors) are the only club to simultaneously win the [Argentine Primera División](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Primera_Divisi%C3%B3n) and the [Copa Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Argentina).

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Boca Juniors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boca_Juniors) | 4 | 1969, [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_in_Argentine_football), [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Club_Atl%C3%A9tico_Boca_Juniors_season), [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Club_Atl%C3%A9tico_Boca_Juniors_season) |

### Bolivia

[Club Bolívar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Club_Bol%C3%ADvar) are the only club to complete the double having won both the [Liga de Fútbol Profesional Boliviano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liga_de_F%C3%BAtbol_Profesional_Boliviano) and [Copa Aerosur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Aerosur) (defunct) in 2009.

### Brazil

Due to the large size of the country and the late development of the transportation and communication infrastructure needed to support it, a nationwide competition did not emerge until 1959. For 30 years, a domestic double was not possible since there existed only one national tournament. An exception was the 1967 season, when [Palmeiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sociedade_Esportiva_Palmeiras) won the double consisting of the inaugural [Torneio Roberto Gomes Pedrosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torneio_Roberto_Gomes_Pedrosa) and the final edition of the [Taça Brasil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta%C3%A7a_Brasil).[^cite_note-6][^cite_note-7]

Since the founding of the [Copa do Brasil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_do_Brasil) in 1989, there has been two teams that won both the Copa do Brasil and the [Campeonato Brasileiro Série A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campeonato_Brasileiro_S%C3%A9rie_A).

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Cruzeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruzeiro_Esporte_Clube) | 1 | 2003 |
| [Atlético-MG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clube_Atl%C3%A9tico_Mineiro) | 1 | [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Clube_Atl%C3%A9tico_Mineiro_season) |

### Canada

Top-level Canadian clubs either compete in the domestic [Canadian Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Premier_League) (CPL), or [Major League Soccer](71802.md) (MLS) of the United States. The [Canadian Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Championship) is the country's national cup and is contested between CPL teams, the three Canadian MLS teams, and the champions of the regional pro-am leagues.

Teams can win the Double by winning both a league championship ([CPL Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Premier_League_Finals) or [MLS Cup](483246.md)) and the [Canadian Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Championship). Teams can also win a league Double, which is achieved by winning both the regular season shield ([CPL Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CPL_Shield) or [Supporters' Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supporters'_Shield)) and the league championship.[^cite_note-Doyle-8]

The only Canadian team to have won a double was [Toronto FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_FC) in 2017, which was also part of a [domestic treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_treble).

During the [original Canadian Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Soccer_League_\(1987%E2%80%931992\)) from 1987 to 1992, there was no formal recognition for the team with the most points at the end of the regular season. However, the [Vancouver 86ers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vancouver_Whitecaps_\(1986%E2%80%932010\)) had the best regular season record and won the league championship in [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Canadian_Soccer_League_season), [1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_Canadian_Soccer_League_season), [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Canadian_Soccer_League_season), and [1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_Canadian_Soccer_League_season).

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Toronto FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_FC) | 1 | [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Toronto_FC_season) (MLS Cup, Canadian Championship) |

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Toronto FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_FC) | 1 | [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Toronto_FC_season) (Supporters' Shield, MLS Cup) |

### United States

#### Men

In the United States, the double consists of the [MLS Cup](483246.md) (Major League Soccer's league championship) and or the [U.S. Open Cup](253156.md) or [Canadian Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Championship) for United States and Canadian clubs, respectively. Teams that win both the [Supporters' Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supporters'_Shield)[^cite_note-Doyle-8] and the MLS Cup are also considered to have won the league double. No team based in the United States has achieved the domestic treble of both MLS honours and a domestic cup in the same season although [Toronto FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_FC), who play in MLS but are [based in Canada](#Canada), won a domestic treble in 2017.

The following is a list of doubles in the United States in the MLS era:

#### Women

There is no currently sanctioned National Cup in the United States for women, so the only way to achieve the double in the United States is through a league double (winning the Shield or its equivalent for the best regular-season record, and the league championship).

The [Women's United Soccer Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_United_Soccer_Association) did not have formal recognition of the team with the best regular season record, but during the [2002 WUSA season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_WUSA_season), the [Carolina Courage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolina_Courage) had the best regular season record and won the [Founders Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WUSA_Founders_Cup), the WUSA Championship Trophy.

[Women's Professional Soccer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_Professional_Soccer), which operated from 2009 to 2011, had one club win both the regular season championship and the WPS Championship (the latter through the playoffs): During the [2011 Women's Professional Soccer season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Women's_Professional_Soccer_season), the [Western New York Flash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_New_York_Flash) had the best regular season record, and won in the Championship match on 27 August 2011 to win the WPS Championship and the double.

The [National Women's Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women's_Soccer_League) did not see a double in its first five seasons (2013–2017). However, in both its 2018 and 2019 seasons, the [North Carolina Courage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Carolina_Courage) won both the [NWSL Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NWSL_Shield) (awarded to the team with the best regular-season record) and the NWSL Championship (earned by winning in the [NWSL Playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NWSL_Playoffs)).

| [Orlando Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando_Pride) | 1 | [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Orlando_Pride_season) |

## Oceania

### Australia

From 1977 to 1997, the double could be achieved in Australia by winning the [National Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Soccer_League) and the [NSL Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSL_Cup).

Between 1997 and 2014, Australia had no national cup competition so the traditional league and cup double could not be achieved. Since the formation of the [A-League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A-League) in 2004, a double could be accomplished by winning the Premiership in the regular league season and the Championship in the finals series. The feat of winning the [minor premiership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minor_premiership) and the [finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playoffs) during the NSL era was not officially recognised as a 'double'.

From [2014–15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_in_Australian_soccer), the [FFA Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FFA_Cup) takes place on an annual basis.

### New Zealand

Between 1970 and 2003, five New Zealand teams have won the double of the national championship ([New Zealand National Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_National_Soccer_League) (1970–92 and 2000–04), [Superclub competition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superclub_competition) (1993–95), [National Summer Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Summer_Soccer_League) (1996–98) or the [New Zealand island soccer leagues National Final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_New_Zealand_island_soccer_leagues#National_Final) (1999)) and the [Chatham Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chatham_Cup). From 1993 to 2003, the league champions were determined by a knockout tournament between the top-finishing teams at the end of the season, the team that finished in first place during the league phase of the season were not officially awarded a title.

From 2003, the league system in the country was drastically restructured and saw the National Soccer League disband, with its teams now competing on a regional basis. The [New Zealand Football Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Football_Championship) was formed and became the country's national league, exclusively run for franchised teams as opposed to the traditional clubs that competed in the National Soccer League.

As with most franchise leagues, the Football Championship was split into two phases; the Premiership during the regular season and the Championship during the finals phase, which was contested between the top four-placed teams of the Premiership phase. The franchises were ineligible to participate in the Chatham Cup. From 2021 onwards this system was replaced by the [New Zealand National League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_National_League), with the Chatham Cup as its primary cup, and the franchises were disbanded.

### Philippines

Due to the sporadic nature of football competitions in the country, with a league not in place until the 21st century, and only the [United Football League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Football_League_\(Philippines\)) (UFL) to last more than a season, and a national cup competition not being consistently held annually, achieving a double has been mostly impossible until recently. The establishment of the UFL in 2009 led to annual league and cup tournaments. The institution of the [PFF National Men's Club Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFF_National_Men's_Club_Championship) (the national cup) in 2011 (next held in 2013) after years of dormancy meant that a domestic treble could now be contested. In 2013, a [super cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_cup) is expected to be played, paving the way for a UFL treble.

During the existence of the UFL from 2009 to 2016, a league double has been achieved thrice. A domestic double (a title each from the UFL and the PFF) or a domestic treble (a UFL double and a PFF title) has not been won.

| Club | Number | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Philippine Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Air_Force_F.C.) | 1 | 2009–10 (UFL Cup and United Football League) |
| [Stallion Sta. Lucia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stallion_Sta._Lucia_F.C.) | 1 | 2012–13 (UFL Cup and United Football League) |
| [Global](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_F.C.) | 1 | 2016 (UFL Cup and United Football League) |
| [Ceres-Negros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceres%E2%80%93Negros_F.C.) | 1 | [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ceres%E2%80%93Negros_F.C._season) (Philippines Football League and Copa Paulino Alcantara) |

## League and league cup double

A rarer (though less coveted) domestic double is that of winning the league championship and the [league cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_cup). In many leagues, this cannot be done as there is no second domestic cup competition (such as in [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serie_A) and the [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eredivisie)) or it has been disbanded (such as in [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga) and [Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_Superliga)). The format of league cups and the number of participating teams can vary enormously from one country to another. In the case of [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Germany), the [DFB-Ligapokal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DFB-Ligapokal) was played in the summer months prior to the [Bundesliga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bundesliga) and therefore was won in the preceding calendar year to the title win.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rangers_F.C.) | [Scotland](26994.md) | 17 | 1947, 1949, 1961, 1964, [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975%E2%80%9376_Rangers_F.C._season), [1978](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1977%E2%80%9378_Rangers_F.C._season), [1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986%E2%80%9387_Rangers_F.C._season), [1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988%E2%80%9389_Rangers_F.C._season), [1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990%E2%80%9391_Rangers_F.C._season), [1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992%E2%80%9393_Rangers_F.C._season), [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993%E2%80%9394_Rangers_F.C._season), [1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996%E2%80%9397_Rangers_F.C._season), [1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998%E2%80%9399_Rangers_F.C._season), [2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%9303_Rangers_F.C._season), [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004%E2%80%9305_Rangers_F.C._season), [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_Rangers_F.C._season), [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_Rangers_F.C._season) | In 1976 as part of a domestic Quadruple with the [Scottish Cup](883238.md) and the [Glasgow Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glasgow_Cup); in 1949, 1964, 1978, 1993, 1999 and 2003 as part of a domestic Treble with the [Scottish Cup](883238.md) |

## Doubles in lower divisions

| icon | This section **does not [cite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Citing_sources) any [sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability)**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Double_\(association_football\)) by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Referencing_for_beginners). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence). *(May 2012)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |

In many countries, knock-out competitions exclusive to clubs outside the top division(s) also exist. This gives lower ranked clubs a chance to win a double. Examples include:

## Trans-state double

A Trans-state double occurs when a club wins a league and a cup which technically belong to two different countries and different associations. This usually happens as a result of a change in the political situation in the club's home country, or if the club has expatriated and is eligible to participate in the competitions of its native country and its adopted one.

## Continental double

### European double

In the same spirit as the European treble, the European double consists of winning the top tier European tournament (currently the [UEFA Champions League](44220.md)) and domestic league title in a single season or calendar year. This has been achieved on 34 occasions by 17 clubs from 56 European competitions. [Barcelona](68187.md) and [Real Madrid](26413.md) has achieved this on the most occasions (5). [Ajax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Ajax), Real Madrid and [Paris Saint-Germain](357488.md) are the only teams that have successfully defended a European double. [José Mourinho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mourinho) has the unique distinction of being one of three managers to achieve this double with two different clubs, having done so with [Porto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Porto) (2004) and [Inter Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter_Milan) (2010). Likewise, career rival [Pep Guardiola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pep_Guardiola) achieved the same feat with [Barcelona](68187.md) (2009) and [Manchester City](165813.md) (2023), as did [Luis Enrique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Enrique) with Barcelona (2015) and Paris Saint-Germain (2025 and 2026). Additionally, both of Guardiola's doubles are also [trebles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)), as Barcelona won the Copa del Rey and City won the FA Cup in their respective seasons in addition to the two trophies. Luis Enrique achieved two trebles as well, with Barcelona in 2015 and Paris Saint-Germain in 2025.

#### The unbeaten double

In 1994–95, [Louis van Gaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_van_Gaal)'s [Ajax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Ajax), with players such as [Jari Litmanen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jari_Litmanen), [Patrick Kluivert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Kluivert), [Marc Overmars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Overmars), [Finidi George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finidi_George), [Nwankwo Kanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nwankwo_Kanu), [Frank de Boer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_de_Boer), [Ronald de Boer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_de_Boer), [Edgar Davids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgar_Davids), [Clarence Seedorf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_Seedorf), [Winston Bogarde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Bogarde), [Michael Reiziger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Reiziger) and [Edwin van der Sar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwin_van_der_Sar), succeeded an unbeaten double, winning the Dutch [Eredivisie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eredivisie) with a 27–7–0 record and the [UEFA Champions League](44220.md) with a 7–4–0 record. Van Gaal's dream team was the only club to achieve a European double with no defeats.

### League and Europa League double

The [UEFA Europa League](232175.md) (formerly the UEFA Cup), with a domestic league title win, offers the chance for a club to win a lesser European double; but arguably a more prestigious achievement than a domestic double. This has been completed on fifteen occasions.

| [IFK Göteborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFK_G%C3%B6teborg) | Sweden | 2 | 1982 (part of a [treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\))), 1987<sup>[\[g\]](#endnote_Note1g)</sup> |

[**<sup>g</sup>**](#ref_Note15g)  In 1987, Göteborg finished third in the [Allsvenskan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allsvenskan). At this time, the title was decided by a play-off between the top four teams. They defeated first placed [Malmö FF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malm%C3%B6_FF) in the play-off final.

### League and Cup Winners' Cup double

Similarly, there were six occasions of clubs winning their League and the now defunct [UEFA Cup Winners' Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup_Winners'_Cup) (CWC). No club ever won the CWC as part of a recognised treble. In the case of [1. FC Magdeburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1._FC_Magdeburg) and [Dynamo Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Dynamo_Kyiv), it is also worth noting that the domestic championships they won are also now disbanded, due to [German reunification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_reunification) and the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union) respectively.

| Club | Country | League | Number won | Year(s) won |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Dynamo Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Dynamo_Kyiv) | Soviet Union | [Soviet Top League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Top_League) | 2 | 1975, 1986 |
| [Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A.C._Milan) | Italy | [Serie A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serie_A) | 1 | [1968](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967%E2%80%9368_A.C._Milan_season) |
| [1. FC Magdeburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1._FC_Magdeburg) | East Germany | [DDR-Oberliga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DDR-Oberliga) | 1 | 1974 |
| [Juventus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juventus_FC) | Italy | [Serie A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serie_A) | 1 | [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983%E2%80%9384_Juventus_FC_season) |
| [Everton](91155.md) | England | [Division One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_League_First_Division) | 1 | [1985](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984%E2%80%9385_Everton_F.C._season) |

## Cup double

There are various possible combinations of winning two knock-out competitions.

### Domestic cup double

A [domestic cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_cup) double consists of winning both domestic cup competitions in a single season or calendar year (for seasons when this double was won in conjunction with the league title, see [domestic treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)#Domestic_trebles)). Examples include:

| Club | Country | Number won | Association Cup | Secondary Cup | Year(s) won | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rangers_F.C.) | [Scotland](26994.md) | 11 | [Scottish Cup](883238.md) | [Scottish League Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_League_Cup) | [1949](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948%E2%80%9349_Rangers_F.C._season), [1962](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1961%E2%80%9362_Rangers_F.C._season), [1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963%E2%80%9364_Rangers_F.C._season), [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975%E2%80%9376_Rangers_F.C._season), [1978](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1977%E2%80%9378_Rangers_F.C._season), [1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978%E2%80%9379_Rangers_F.C._season), [1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992%E2%80%9393_Rangers_F.C._season), [1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998%E2%80%9399_Rangers_F.C._season), [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001%E2%80%9302_Rangers_F.C._season), [2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%9303_Rangers_F.C._season), [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007%E2%80%9308_Rangers_F.C._season) | In 1976 as part of a domestic quadruple with the [Scottish League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Scottish_football_champions) and the [Glasgow Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glasgow_Cup); in 1949, 1964, 1978, 1993, 1999 and 2003 as part of a domestic treble with the [Scottish League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Scottish_football_champions) |

### European cup double

There have been several occasions when a club has won its association's cup or league cup and a UEFA trophy but not its League title. For European cup doubles won in conjunction with the league title, see the [treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)).

## Other

A combination of domestic league or cup and a lesser domestic or continental trophy may be won in the same season. Examples include (this list does not include doubles achieved as part of a treble):

### UEFA

- [Barcelona](68187.md) won [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga) and the [Inter-Cities Fairs Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-Cities_Fairs_Cup) in 1959–60, and the [UEFA Cup Winners Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup_Winners_Cup) and [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_del_Rey) in 1996–97.

- [Zaragoza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Zaragoza) won an [Inter-Cities Fairs Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-Cities_Fairs_Cup) and [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_del_Rey) double in the 1963–64 season.

- [Leeds United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leeds_United_F.C.) were the first English team to win a double involving European and domestic trophies. It was in 1968 when Leeds won the [Football League Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Football_League_Cup_Final) and [Inter-Cities Fairs Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967%E2%80%9368_Inter-Cities_Fairs_Cup).

  [^cite_note-Kry1-15]

- [Manchester City](165813.md) won the [UEFA Cup Winners Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup_Winners_Cup) and [Football League Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_League_Cup) in [1969–70](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969%E2%80%9370_Manchester_City_F.C._season).

- [Nottingham Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nottingham_Forest_F.C.) won the [European Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Cup) and [Football League Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_League_Cup) in [1978–79](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978%E2%80%9379_Nottingham_Forest_F.C._season), and the Football League Cup and [Full Members' Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Full_Members'_Cup) in [1988–89](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988%E2%80%9389_Nottingham_Forest_F.C._season).

- [Aberdeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeen_F.C.) won the [UEFA Cup Winners Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup_Winners_Cup) and [Scottish Cup](883238.md) in [1982–83](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982%E2%80%9383_Aberdeen_F.C._season).

- [Real Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid) won the [UEFA Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup) and [Copa de la Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_de_la_Liga) in [1984–85](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984%E2%80%9385_Real_Madrid_CF_season).

- [Dinamo Tbilisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Dinamo_Tbilisi) won the [Georgian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_Cup) and [CIS Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIS_Cup) in 2004.

- [Espanyol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCD_Espanyol) won the [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_del_Rey) and [Copa Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Catalunya) in 2006. This would not normally be counted as a domestic cup double comparable to those mentioned above, as the latter competition is not nationwide or league-wide.

- [Copenhagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F.C._Copenhagen) won the [Danish Superliga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_Superliga) and the pan-Scandinavian [Royal League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_League) in [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_F.C._Copenhagen_season).

- [Sevilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sevilla_F.C.) won the [UEFA Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup) and [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_del_Rey) in 2006–07.

- [FBK Kaunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FBK_Kaunas) won the [Lithuanian Football Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Football_Cup) and [Baltic League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_League) in 2008.

- [Chelsea](7473.md) won the [UEFA Champions League](44220.md) and [FA Cup](11237.md) in [2011–12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Chelsea_F.C._season)

- [Manchester United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_United_F.C.) won the [UEFA Europa League](232175.md) and [EFL Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EFL_Cup) in [2016–17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Manchester_United_F.C._season).

### AFC

- [Al-Quwa Al-Jawiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quwa_Al-Jawiya) won the [Iraq FA Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_FA_Cup) and [AFC Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Cup) in 2016.

- [Al-Quwa Al-Jawiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quwa_Al-Jawiya) won the [Iraqi Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Premier_League) and [AFC Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Cup) in 2017.

- [Mumbai City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_City_FC) won the [ISL League Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_Indian_Super_League#Regular_season) and [ISL Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Indian_Super_League_playoffs) in the [2020–21 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_Mumbai_City_FC_season).

- [Mohun Bagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohun_Bagan_Super_Giant) won the [Durand Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Durand_Cup) and [ISL League Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_Indian_Super_League) in the [2023–24 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_Mohun_Bagan_Super_Giant_season).

- [Mohun Bagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohun_Bagan_Super_Giant) won the [ISL League Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_Indian_Super_League) and [ISL Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Indian_Super_League_playoffs) in the [2024–25 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_Mohun_Bagan_Super_Giant_season).

## International double

A national team's continental championship is not held in the same year as the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) (although the [Africa Cup of Nations](250680.md) was held in the same year as the World Cup up until 2010). A team which wins both titles consecutively is said to have "done the double".

### World and continental champions

-  [Uruguay](679782.md) won the gold medal at the [1924 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1924_Summer_Olympics) and the [1924 South American Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1924_South_American_Championship). (Prior to the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md)'s foundation in 1930, the Olympic champions were considered to be the world champions).

-  [West Germany](250204.md) won [UEFA Euro 1972](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_1972) and the [1974 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup)

-  [France](250197.md) won the [1998 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup) and [UEFA Euro 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2000)

-  [Brazil](149286.md) won the [2002 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup) and the [2004 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica)

-  [Spain](609825.md) won [UEFA Euro 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2008), the [2010 FIFA World Cup](168079.md), and [UEFA Euro 2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2012)

-  [Argentina](454699.md) won the [2021 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica), the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md) and the [2024 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica)

-  [Spain](609825.md) won [UEFA Euro 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024) and the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md)

### The International 'Treble'

Only two teams in history have gone on to win three consecutive major international trophies.

-  [Spain](609825.md), by winning [UEFA Euro 2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2012), became the first team in history to win three major titles consecutively, having previously won [UEFA Euro 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2008), and the [2010 FIFA World Cup](168079.md)

-  [Argentina](454699.md) won the [2021 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica), the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md), and the [2024 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica) to become the first South American team to win three international titles in a row.

### World and other tournament champions

-  [Italy](362466.md) won the [1933–35 Central European International Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1933%E2%80%9335_Central_European_International_Cup) and the [1934 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1934_FIFA_World_Cup)

-  [Brazil](149286.md) won the [1956 Panamerican Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panamerican_Championship_1956) and the [1958 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1958_FIFA_World_Cup)

-  [England](9904.md) won the [1965–66 British Home Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1965%E2%80%9366_British_Home_Championship) and the [1966 FIFA World Cup](61629.md)

-  [Germany](250204.md) won the [2014 FIFA World Cup](656933.md) and the [2017 FIFA Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_FIFA_Confederations_Cup)

-  [France](250197.md) won the [2018 FIFA World Cup](13327177.md) and the [2020–21 UEFA Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_UEFA_Nations_League)

-  [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany_women's_national_football_team) won [2003 FIFA Women's World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_FIFA_Women's_World_Cup) and the [UEFA Women's Euro 2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Women's_Euro_2005). They repeated again when they won [2007 FIFA Women's World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_FIFA_Women's_World_Cup) and the [UEFA Women's Euro 2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Women's_Euro_2009)

-  [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spain_women's_national_football_team) won [2023 FIFA Women's World Cup](45271353.md) and the [2023–24 UEFA Women's Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_UEFA_Women's_Nations_League)

### Continental and other tournament champions

-  [Iran](272865.md) won the [1970 RCD Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_RCD_Cup) and the [1972 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_AFC_Asian_Cup)

-  [Ghana](1029620.md) won the [1982 African Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_African_Cup_of_Nations), [1982 West African Nations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_West_African_Nations_Cup), and [1983 West African Nations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_West_African_Nations_Cup)

-  [Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denmark_national_football_team) won the [1981–85 Nordic Football Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981%E2%80%9385_Nordic_Football_Championship) and [UEFA Euro 1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_1992). (The Nordic Football Championship was not played between 1985 and 2001, so Denmark were still the holders.)

-  [Ivory Coast](1039856.md) won the [1991 CEDEAO Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CEDEAO_Cup#1991_CEDEAO_Cup), and the [1992 African Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_African_Cup_of_Nations)

-  [Argentina](454699.md) won the [1991 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica), the [1992 King Fahd Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_King_Fahd_Cup) and the [1993 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica)

-  [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team) won the [1996 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_AFC_Asian_Cup) and the [1998 Arab Nations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Arab_Nations_Cup)

-  [Brazil](149286.md) won the [1997 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica) and the [1997 FIFA Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_FIFA_Confederations_Cup)

-  [Mexico](808402.md) won the [1998 CONCACAF Gold Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_CONCACAF_Gold_Cup) and the [1999 FIFA Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_FIFA_Confederations_Cup)

-  [Cameroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameroon_national_football_team) won the [2000 African Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_African_Cup_of_Nations), the [2002 African Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_African_Cup_of_Nations), and the [2003 CEMAC Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_CEMAC_Cup)

-  [Iraq](1039652.md) won the [2005 West Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2005_West_Asian_Games) and the [2007 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup)

-  [Brazil](149286.md) won the [2007 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica) and the [2009 FIFA Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_FIFA_Confederations_Cup)

-  [Japan](993546.md) won the [2011 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_AFC_Asian_Cup) and the [2013 EAFF East Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_EAFF_East_Asian_Cup)

-  [Portugal](576328.md) won [UEFA Euro 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2016) and the [2018–19 UEFA Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_UEFA_Nations_League)

-  [Senegal](1017436.md) won the [2019 WAFU Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WAFU_Cup_of_Nations) and the [2021 Africa Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Africa_Cup_of_Nations)

-  [United States](582488.md) won the [2019–20 CONCACAF Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_CONCACAF_Nations_League) and the [2021 CONCACAF Gold Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_CONCACAF_Gold_Cup)

-  [Algeria](1029528.md) won the [2019 Africa Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Africa_Cup_of_Nations) and the [2021 FIFA Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_FIFA_Arab_Cup)

-  [Spain](609825.md) won the [2022–23 UEFA Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%9323_UEFA_Nations_League) and [UEFA Euro 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024)

-  [Mexico](808402.md) won the [2024–25 CONCACAF Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_CONCACAF_Nations_League) and the [2025 CONCACAF Gold Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_CONCACAF_Gold_Cup)

## Double winning managers

| icon | This section **does not [cite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Citing_sources) any [sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability)**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Double_\(association_football\)) by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Referencing_for_beginners). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence). *(May 2012)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |

- As manager of [Preston North End](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preston_North_End_F.C.) in 1888–89, [William Sudell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Sudell) won the inaugural [First Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_League_Division_One) title, going unbeaten in the league (22 games), and won the [FA Cup](11237.md) without conceding a goal in that competition.

- [Jock Stein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jock_Stein) won the double on nine occasions with [Celtic](69708.md) between 1967 and 1977: four doubles of the [Scottish league championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Scottish_football_champions) and the [Scottish Cup](883238.md), three doubles of the [Scottish league championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Scottish_football_champions) and the [Scottish League Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_League_Cup) and two [Trebles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)).

- [Sir Alex Ferguson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Ferguson) won the double on four occasions; with [Aberdeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeen_F.C.) in 1983–84, and with [Manchester United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_United_F.C.) in 1993–94, 1995–96 and 1998–99. This achievement makes him the only manager to win the double on both sides of the [Anglo-Scottish border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Scottish_border).

- Two managers won the domestic double in three different countries:&#x20;

  - [Sven-Göran Eriksson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sven-G%C3%B6ran_Eriksson), in Sweden, Portugal and Italy with [IFK Göteborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFK_G%C3%B6teborg) (1981–82), [Benfica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.L._Benfica) (1982–83) and [Lazio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.S._Lazio) (1999–2000) respectively. This included back-to-back double wins in separate countries.

  - [Pep Guardiola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pep_Guardiola), in Spain, Germany and England with [Barcelona](68187.md) (2008–09), [Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich) (2013–14 and 2015–16) and [Manchester City](165813.md) (2018–19 and 2022–23)

- [Louis van Gaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_van_Gaal) won the double with [Barcelona](68187.md) in 1997–98 and [Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich) in 2009–10, subsequent to his undefeated European Double with [Ajax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Ajax) in 1994–95.

- [Massimiliano Allegri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massimiliano_Allegri) became the first manager in Italy to win the double four consecutive times, in 2014–15, 2015–16, 2016–17 and 2017–18, all with [Juventus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juventus_FC).
